I haven't really noticed it happening at home or when I'm out with my friends. I guess I'm mostly just aware of it at work because that's where I keep getting in trouble for it. But yeah, outside of the plant, I haven't really paid attention to feeling like that, so I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Are you having problems with friends or family because of this behavior?\n\nPatient: No, not really with my family or friends. It's mostly just a problem at work. Like I said, I keep getting in trouble... My supervisors have actually written me up a few times because me walking away causes safety issues and slows down production. It's gotten to the point where it's putting my job on the line, and it's definitely messing up my plans to get certified. Plus, my coworkers are getting annoyed. They're a lot less willing to partner with me during handoffs now because they know I might just wander off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted or having trouble paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially when I'm on the line. It's like... just one beep from another machine, or somebody walking past with a pallet jack, and it will pull my eyes and my whole brain right away from what I'm doing. Even if I know I'm supposed to stay focused on my own station, I just... my attention just goes right to whatever that noise or movement was.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling anxious or stressed about the problems you're having at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Once I clock out, I kind of just leave it all there. I don't really sit around at home stressing about getting written up or anything. I mean, it sucks when I'm actually at the plant and getting in trouble, but... yeah, as soon as I leave, I'm just onto whatever else I'm doing. I don't really worry about it when I'm not there.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any problems with friends or family because they are worried about you or because of how you've been acting when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, my family and friends aren't really having any issues with me. They don't seem worried or annoyed or anything. It's really just a work thing. When I'm hanging out with friends or at home, everything's pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody really likes waiting in line, but... I just stand there and wait my turn. It doesn't really bother me or make me feel crazy or anything. So, yeah, I haven't really noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble doing things quietly, like reading or drawing, without getting up or making noise?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have trouble with that. Quiet stuff kind of makes me feel trapped, honestly. Like, if I try to just sit in the break room and read a manual or something, I can't just sit there quietly. I end up humming or tapping my foot, or I'll just start talking to whoever else is in there. Or I just get up and do something else instead of just being quiet. I just can't really do the quiet thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do that a lot. I cut in on people without really meaning to. Like, if my coworkers are talking, I'll just jump right into their conversation. Or if the shift lead is asking a question, I'll just blurt out the answer before they're even done talking. It just happens. And then everybody gets irritated with me because I didn't wait for them to finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family because of your behavior, such as interrupting or restlessness, when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Honestly, my family is mostly just used to it, so it's not a huge deal at home. But it's actually causing major problems for me at work. It's gotten bad enough that it's putting my job and my certification plans at risk. My supervisors have already written me up for safety and production problems. And my coworkers... they're getting a lot less willing to partner with me during shift handoffs because of it all. So, yeah, work is where it's really messing things up for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, my supervisor actually told me I needed to come in. I work in production, and the main problem is I just keep leaving my station. Like, I'll just pop up and walk away when I'm supposed to stay put, or I'll get out of my chair right in the middle of our shift meetings. My boss says it's becoming a real safety problem because I just take off before I even think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about leaving your station before you actually get up, or does it just happen without you thinking about it first?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't think I plan it out or anything. It's more like... I just find myself already up and walking. I don't really sit there and tell myself to leave. It just happens without me thinking about it first, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety before you get up and walk away from your station?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... when I try to just stand still by my machine, or if I have to sit through a training, it feels like there's this pressure building up inside my body. And I just have to move. Like, I need to pace, or stretch, or just go do something right then. I can't really ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this restlessness or need to move when you're not at work, like when you're at home or out with friends?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it happening at home or when I'm out with my friends. I guess I'm mostly just aware of it at work because that's where I keep getting in trouble for it. But yeah, outside of the plant, I haven't really paid attention to feeling like that, so I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Are you having problems with friends or family because of this behavior?\n\nPatient: No, not really with my family or friends. It's mostly just a problem at work. Like I said, I keep getting in trouble... My supervisors have actually written me up a few times because me walking away causes safety issues and slows down production. It's gotten to the point where it's putting my job on the line, and it's definitely messing up my plans to get certified. Plus, my coworkers are getting annoyed. They're a lot less willing to partner with me during handoffs now because they know I might just wander off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted or having trouble paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially when I'm on the line. It's like... just one beep from another machine, or somebody walking past with a pallet jack, and it will pull my eyes and my whole brain right away from what I'm doing. Even if I know I'm supposed to stay focused on my own station, I just... my attention just goes right to whatever that noise or movement was.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling anxious or stressed about the problems you're having at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Once I clock out, I kind of just leave it all there. I don't really sit around at home stressing about getting written up or anything. I don't really worry about it when I'm not there.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any problems with friends or family because they are worried about you or because of how you've been acting when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, my supervisor actually told me I needed to come in. I work in production, and the main problem is I just keep leaving my station. Like, I'll just pop up and walk away when I'm supposed to stay put, or I'll get out of my chair right in the middle of our shift meetings. My boss says it's becoming a real safety problem because I just take off before I even think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about leaving your station before you actually get up, or does it just happen without you thinking about it first?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't think I plan it out or anything. It's more like... I just find myself already up and walking. I don't really sit there and tell myself to leave. It just happens without me thinking about it first, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety before you get up and walk away from your station?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... when I try to just stand still by my machine, or if I have to sit through a training, it feels like there's this pressure building up inside my body. And I just have to move. Like, I need to pace, or stretch, or just go do something right then. I can't really ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this restlessness or need to move when you're not at work, like when you're at home or out with friends?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it happening at home or when I'm out with my friends. I guess I'm mostly just aware of it at work because that's where I keep getting in trouble for it. But yeah, outside of the plant, I haven't really paid attention to feeling like that, so I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Are you having problems with friends or family because of this behavior?\n\nPatient: No, not really with my family or friends. It's mostly just a problem at work. Like I said, I keep getting in trouble... My supervisors have actually written me up a few times because me walking away causes safety issues and slows down production. It's gotten to the point where it's putting my job on the line, and it's definitely messing up my plans to get certified. Plus, my coworkers are getting annoyed. They're a lot less willing to partner with me during handoffs now because they know I might just wander off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted or having trouble paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially when I'm on the line. It's like... just one beep from another machine, or somebody walking past with a pallet jack, and it will pull my eyes and my whole brain right away from what I'm doing. Even if I know I'm supposed to stay focused on my own station, I just... my attention just goes right to whatever that noise or movement was.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling anxious or stressed about the problems you're having at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Once I clock out, I kind of just leave it all there. I don't really sit around at home stressing about getting written up or anything. I mean, it sucks when I'm actually at the plant and getting in trouble, but... yeah, as soon as I leave, I'm just onto whatever else I'm doing. I don't really worry about it when I'm not there.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any problems with friends or family because they are worried about you or because of how you've been acting when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, my family and friends aren't really having any issues with me. They don't seem worried or annoyed or anything. It's really just a work thing. When I'm hanging out with friends or at home, everything's pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody really likes waiting in line, but... I just stand there and wait my turn. It doesn't really bother me or make me feel crazy or anything. So, yeah, I haven't really noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble doing things quietly, like reading or drawing, without getting up or making noise?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have trouble with that. Quiet stuff kind of makes me feel trapped, honestly. Like, if I try to just sit in the break room and read a manual or something, I can't just sit there quietly. I end up humming or tapping my foot, or I'll just start talking to whoever else is in there. Or I just get up and do something else instead of just being quiet. I just can't really do the quiet thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do that a lot. I cut in on people without really meaning to. Like, if my coworkers are talking, I'll just jump right into their conversation. Or if the shift lead is asking a question, I'll just blurt out the answer before they're even done talking. It just happens. And then everybody gets irritated with me because I didn't wait for them to finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family because of your behavior, such as interrupting or restlessness, when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Honestly, my family is mostly just used to it, so it's not a huge deal at home. But it's actually causing major problems for me at work. It's gotten bad enough that it's putting my job and my certification plans at risk. My supervisors have already written me up for safety and production problems. And my coworkers... they're getting a lot less willing to partner with me during shift handoffs because of it all. So, yeah, work is where it's really messing things up for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Outside of work, I don't really have to organize much. I just kind of do things as they come up, so I haven't noticed any trouble with that. It's really just the work stuff that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless or have trouble sitting still when you're watching TV or a movie at home?\n\nPatient: Not really when I'm watching TV at home, because I can just get up whenever I want. But at work, yeah, definitely. I just have to move, or pace, or stretch, or go do something right then. I just can't stay still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have these problems with restlessness and leaving your spot if you were working in a different job or setting?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I've only really done this kind of factory work since I left school, so I don't have much else to compare it to. I guess maybe if I had a job where I was moving around all the time anyway it wouldn't be as big of a deal, but I've never tried anything else. So I'm not really sure if it would happen somewhere else or not.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. My boss says it's becoming a real safety problem because I just take off before I even think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about leaving your station before you actually get up, or does it just happen without you thinking about it first?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't think I plan it out or anything. It's more like... I just find myself already up and walking. I don't really sit there and tell myself to leave. It just happens without me thinking about it first, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety before you get up and walk away from your station?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... when I try to just stand still by my machine, or if I have to sit through a training, it feels like there's this pressure building up inside my body. And I just have to move. Like, I need to pace, or stretch, or just go do something right then. I can't really ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this restlessness or need to move when you're not at work, like when you're at home or out with friends?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it happening at home or when I'm out with my friends. I guess I'm mostly just aware of it at work because that's where I keep getting in trouble for it. But yeah, outside of the plant, I haven't really paid attention to feeling like that, so I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Are you having problems with friends or family because of this behavior?\n\nPatient: No, not really with my family or friends. It's mostly just a problem at work. Like I said, I keep getting in trouble... My supervisors have actually written me up a few times because me walking away causes safety issues and slows down production. It's gotten to the point where it's putting my job on the line, and it's definitely messing up my plans to get certified. Plus, my coworkers are getting annoyed. They're a lot less willing to partner with me during handoffs now because they know I might just wander off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted or having trouble paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially when I'm on the line. It's like... just one beep from another machine, or somebody walking past with a pallet jack, and it will pull my eyes and my whole brain right away from what I'm doing. Even if I know I'm supposed to stay focused on my own station, I just... my attention just goes right to whatever that noise or movement was.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling anxious or stressed about the problems you're having at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Once I clock out, I kind of just leave it all there. I don't really sit around at home stressing about getting written up or anything. I mean, it sucks when I'm actually at the plant and getting in trouble, but... yeah, as soon as I leave, I'm just onto whatever else I'm doing. I don't really worry about it when I'm not there.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any problems with friends or family because they are worried about you or because of how you've been acting when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, my family and friends aren't really having any issues with me. They don't seem worried or annoyed or anything. It's really just a work thing. When I'm hanging out with friends or at home, everything's pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody really likes waiting in line, but... I just stand there and wait my turn. It doesn't really bother me or make me feel crazy or anything. So, yeah, I haven't really noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble doing things quietly, like reading or drawing, without getting up or making noise?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have trouble with that. Quiet stuff kind of makes me feel trapped, honestly. Like, if I try to just sit in the break room and read a manual or something, I can't just sit there quietly. I end up humming or tapping my foot, or I'll just start talking to whoever else is in there. Or I just get up and do something else instead of just being quiet. I just can't really do the quiet thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do that a lot. I cut in on people without really meaning to. Like, if my coworkers are talking, I'll just jump right into their conversation. Or if the shift lead is asking a question, I'll just blurt out the answer before they're even done talking. It just happens. And then everybody gets irritated with me because I didn't wait for them to finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family because of your behavior, such as interrupting or restlessness, when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Honestly, my family is mostly just used to it, so it's not a huge deal at home. So, yeah, work is where it's really messing things up for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just can't stay still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have these problems with restlessness and leaving your spot if you were working in a different job or setting?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I've only really done this kind of factory work since I left school, so I don't have much else to compare it to. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, my supervisor actually told me I needed to come in. I work in production, and the main problem is I just keep leaving my station. Like, I'll just pop up and walk away when I'm supposed to stay put, or I'll get out of my chair right in the middle of our shift meetings. My boss says it's becoming a real safety problem because I just take off before I even think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about leaving your station before you actually get up, or does it just happen without you thinking about it first?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't think I plan it out or anything. It's more like... I just find myself already up and walking. I don't really sit there and tell myself to leave. It just happens without me thinking about it first, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or fidgety before you get up and walk away from your station?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... when I try to just stand still by my machine, or if I have to sit through a training, it feels like there's this pressure building up inside my body. And I just have to move. Like, I need to pace, or stretch, or just go do something right then. I can't really ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience this restlessness or need to move when you're not at work, like when you're at home or out with friends?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it happening at home or when I'm out with my friends. I guess I'm mostly just aware of it at work because that's where I keep getting in trouble for it. But yeah, outside of the plant, I haven't really paid attention to feeling like that, so I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Are you having problems with friends or family because of this behavior?\n\nPatient: No, not really with my family or friends. It's mostly just a problem at work. Like I said, I keep getting in trouble... My supervisors have actually written me up a few times because me walking away causes safety issues and slows down production. It's gotten to the point where it's putting my job on the line, and it's definitely messing up my plans to get certified. Plus, my coworkers are getting annoyed. They're a lot less willing to partner with me during handoffs now because they know I might just wander off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted or having trouble paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially when I'm on the line. It's like... just one beep from another machine, or somebody walking past with a pallet jack, and it will pull my eyes and my whole brain right away from what I'm doing. Even if I know I'm supposed to stay focused on my own station, I just... my attention just goes right to whatever that noise or movement was.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling anxious or stressed about the problems you're having at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Once I clock out, I kind of just leave it all there. I don't really sit around at home stressing about getting written up or anything. I mean, it sucks when I'm actually at the plant and getting in trouble, but... yeah, as soon as I leave, I'm just onto whatever else I'm doing. I don't really worry about it when I'm not there.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any problems with friends or family because they are worried about you or because of how you've been acting when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, my family and friends aren't really having any issues with me. They don't seem worried or annoyed or anything. It's really just a work thing. When I'm hanging out with friends or at home, everything's pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody really likes waiting in line, but... I just stand there and wait my turn. It doesn't really bother me or make me feel crazy or anything. So, yeah, I haven't really noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble doing things quietly, like reading or drawing, without getting up or making noise?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have trouble with that. Quiet stuff kind of makes me feel trapped, honestly. Like, if I try to just sit in the break room and read a manual or something, I can't just sit there quietly. I end up humming or tapping my foot, or I'll just start talking to whoever else is in there. Or I just get up and do something else instead of just being quiet. I just can't really do the quiet thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do that a lot. I cut in on people without really meaning to. Like, if my coworkers are talking, I'll just jump right into their conversation. Or if the shift lead is asking a question, I'll just blurt out the answer before they're even done talking. It just happens. And then everybody gets irritated with me because I didn't wait for them to finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family because of your behavior, such as interrupting or restlessness, when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Honestly, my family is mostly just used to it, so it's not a huge deal at home. But it's actually causing major problems for me at work. It's gotten bad enough that it's putting my job and my certification plans at risk. My supervisors have already written me up for safety and production problems. And my coworkers... they're getting a lot less willing to partner with me during shift handoffs because of it all. So, yeah, work is where it's really messing things up for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Outside of work, I don't really have to organize much. I just kind of do things as they come up, so I haven't noticed any trouble with that. It's really just the work stuff that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless or have trouble sitting still when you're watching TV or a movie at home?\n\nPatient: Not really when I'm watching TV at home, because I can just get up whenever I want. But at work, yeah, definitely. When I try to stand still by the machine, or if I have to sit through one of those training sessions, it just... it feels like there's this pressure building up inside my body. I just have to move, or pace, or stretch, or go do something right then. I just can't stay still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have these problems with restlessness and leaving your spot if you were working in a different job or setting?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I've only really done this kind of factory work since I left school, so I don't have much else to compare it to. I guess maybe if I had a job where I was moving around all the time anyway it wouldn't be as big of a deal, but I've never tried anything else. So I'm not really sure if it would happen somewhere else or not.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or paying attention when you were a kid in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely did. Even back in elementary school, I was always that kid who just couldn't stay in my seat. I was always talking over the teacher, and I'd get notes sent home to my parents all the time because I was blurting things out or just getting up and wandering around the classroom. As far back as I can remember, honestly. Even in early elementary school I was always moving around and getting in trouble for it, so, yeah, it's basically been my whole life.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. They're a lot less willing to partner with me during handoffs now because they know I might just wander off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted or having trouble paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's like... just one beep from another machine, or somebody walking past with a pallet jack, and it will pull my eyes and my whole brain right away from what I'm doing. Once I clock out, I kind of just leave it all there. I don't really sit around at home stressing about getting written up or anything. I mean, it sucks when I'm actually at the plant and getting in trouble, but... yeah, as soon as I leave, I'm just onto whatever else I'm doing. I don't really worry about it when I'm not there.\n\nDoctor: Are you having any problems with friends or family because they are worried about you or because of how you've been acting when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, my family and friends aren't really having any issues with me. They don't seem worried or annoyed or anything. It's really just a work thing. When I'm hanging out with friends or at home, everything's pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody really likes waiting in line, but... I just stand there and wait my turn. It doesn't really bother me or make me feel crazy or anything. So, yeah, I haven't really noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble doing things quietly, like reading or drawing, without getting up or making noise?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have trouble with that. Quiet stuff kind of makes me feel trapped, honestly. Like, if I try to just sit in the break room and read a manual or something, I can't just sit there quietly. I end up humming or tapping my foot, or I'll just start talking to whoever else is in there. Or I just get up and do something else instead of just being quiet. I just can't really do the quiet thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are talking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do that a lot. I cut in on people without really meaning to. It just happens. So, yeah, work is where it's really messing things up for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's really just the work stuff that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless or have trouble sitting still when you're watching TV or a movie at home?\n\nPatient: Not really when I'm watching TV at home, because I can just get up whenever I want. I just have to move, or pace, or stretch, or go do something right then. I just can't stay still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have these problems with restlessness and leaving your spot if you were working in a different job or setting?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I've only really done this kind of factory work since I left school, so I don't have much else to compare it to. I guess maybe if I had a job where I was moving around all the time anyway it wouldn't be as big of a deal, but I've never tried anything else.
